People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages are specifically intended for you, and not for anyone else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound foolish, doctor, but yes. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it does come back, especially with the men at the domino table or when I hear the radio announcer. There are times I’m not thinking about it, sure, but when they say those little things, it feels like the message is meant for me. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Not every day, and not all the time, you know, it comes and goes. But those voices and the little messages… they’ve been happening on and off for around nine months now, I’d say. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I don’t want to make it sound like a big problem… but I came because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, they say little things that feel like they’re meant for me. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. Even if others say it isn’t, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. It’s mostly what I already told you, about the men at the domino table and sometimes the radio announcer. They say these little things, and it feels like the messages are meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try not to make a big thing of it… but I do feel they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I try to listen to her, but I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there are other things going on that are specifically about you or directed at you, besides these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say there’s a lot else, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound strange. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I don’t want to make it sound like a big problem… but I came because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, they say little things that feel like they’re meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just regular talk, and maybe I shouldn’t worry so much, but… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear them say your name or directly address you, or is it more that you interpret their words as being about you?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly my name, doctor… not like they turn and say, “Hey, you.” It’s more that what they say, the words they choose, it feels meant for me. Like at the domino table, or on the radio, there are little messages in it. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, but I still feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages are specifically intended for you, and not for anyone else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound foolish, doctor, but yes. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it does come back, especially with the men at the domino table or when I hear the radio announcer. There are times I’m not thinking about it, sure, but when they say those little things, it feels like the message is meant for me. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe these messages are meant for you even when your wife or others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do, though I know maybe it sounds strange. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, I really do. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I don’t want to make it sound like a big problem… but I came because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, they say little things that feel like they’re meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just regular talk, and maybe I shouldn’t worry so much, but… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear them say your name or directly address you, or is it more that you interpret their words as being about you?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly my name, doctor… not like they turn and say, “Hey, you.” It’s more that what they say, the words they choose, it feels meant for me. Like at the domino table, or on the radio, there are little messages in it. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, but I still feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages are specifically intended for you, and not for anyone else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound foolish, doctor, but yes. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it does come back, especially with the men at the domino table or when I hear the radio announcer. There are times I’m not thinking about it, sure, but when they say those little things, it feels like the message is meant for me. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Not every day, and not all the time, you know, it comes and goes. But those voices and the little messages… they’ve been happening on and off for around nine months now, I’d say. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe these messages are meant for you even when your wife or others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do, though I know maybe it sounds strange. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, I really do. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. But doctor, that’s why I came—those men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they give these little messages, and it feels meant for me. Even if others say it isn’t, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try not to make a big thing of it… but I do feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say… harm me exactly, doctor. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I try to listen to her, but I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there are other things going on that are specifically about you or directed at you, besides these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say there’s a lot else, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound strange. They’ll say something, and it feels like it’s for me, like they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen, but… I still feel it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these beliefs about the messages ever completely go away for a long time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try not to dwell on it, doctor, and sometimes I can go on with my day and not think about it much. My wife says it’s just talk, and I want to believe her, but I still know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think maybe I could stop, doctor… I don’t want to be difficult about it. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say… harm me exactly, doctor. They’ll say something, and it feels like it’s for me, like they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen, but… I still feel it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these beliefs about the messages ever completely go away for a long time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try not to dwell on it, doctor, and sometimes I can go on with my day and not think about it much. But it doesn’t really go away for a long time. When the men at the domino table say those things, or the radio announcer says something, I still feel it’s meant for me… like they’re warning me to be careful, even if my wife tells me it’s just talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages for several weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Several weeks? No, doctor, I don’t think so… I try not to make too much of it, and some days I keep busy and don’t think on it as much. But it’s still there, you know. The men at the domino table, or sometimes the radio announcer, they say little things and I feel it’s meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and I want to believe her, but I still know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think maybe I could stop, doctor… I don’t want to be difficult about it. Even when my wife says it’s just talk, I can’t quite let go of it… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages will continue to feel meant for you in the future?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, doctor, I think they probably will. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. But doctor, that’s why I came—those men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they give these little messages, and it feels meant for me. Even if others say it isn’t, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. It’s mostly what I already told you, about the men at the domino table and sometimes the radio announcer. They say these little things, and it feels like the messages are meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try not to make a big thing of it… but I do feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say… harm me exactly, doctor. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s more that the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, say little things that feel meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I try to listen to her, but I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there are other things going on that are specifically about you or directed at you, besides these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say there’s a lot else, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound strange. But it doesn’t really go away for a long time. When the men at the domino table say those things, or the radio announcer says something, I still feel it’s meant for me… like they’re warning me to be careful, even if my wife tells me it’s just talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages for several weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Several weeks? My wife says it’s just talk, and I want to believe her, but I still know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think maybe I could stop, doctor… I don’t want to be difficult about it. My wife tells me it’s just regular talk, and maybe I shouldn’t worry so much, but… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear them say your name or directly address you, or is it more that you interpret their words as being about you?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly my name, doctor… not like they turn and say, “Hey, you.” It’s more that what they say, the words they choose, it feels meant for me. Like at the domino table, or on the radio, there are little messages in it. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, but I still feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages are specifically intended for you, and not for anyone else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound foolish, doctor, but yes. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it does come back, especially with the men at the domino table or when I hear the radio announcer. There are times I’m not thinking about it, sure, but when they say those little things, it feels like the message is meant for me. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Not every day, and not all the time, you know, it comes and goes. But those voices and the little messages… they’ve been happening on and off for around nine months now, I’d say. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe these messages are meant for you even when your wife or others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do, though I know maybe it sounds strange. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, I really do. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. But doctor, that’s why I came—those men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they give these little messages, and it feels meant for me. Even if others say it isn’t, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say… harm me exactly, doctor. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I try to listen to her, but I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there are other things going on that are specifically about you or directed at you, besides these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say there’s a lot else, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound strange. They’ll say something, and it feels like it’s for me, like they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen, but… I still feel it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these beliefs about the messages ever completely go away for a long time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try not to dwell on it, doctor, and sometimes I can go on with my day and not think about it much. Even when my wife says it’s just talk, I can’t quite let go of it… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages will continue to feel meant for you in the future?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, doctor, I think they probably will. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe these messages are meant for you even when your wife or others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do, though I know maybe it sounds strange. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, I really do. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. I came because it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. Is that alright to say?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your beliefs about the messages will ever change?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 84 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"Doctor, I came because the men at the domino table and sometimes the radio announcer give little messages meant for me, and even when my wife says it is just talk, I know they are warning me to be careful.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d like to say yes, doctor, that they’ll change and I’ll stop worrying over it so much. I don’t want to sound stubborn about it. But… when the men at the domino table say those things, or the radio announcer says something just right, it still feels like it’s meant for me. Even when my wife tells me it’s just talk, I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.

Maybe with your help it could change some… is that okay to say?

My wife tells me it’s just regular talk, and maybe I shouldn’t worry so much, but… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you actually hear them say your name or directly address you, or is it more that you interpret their words as being about you?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly my name, doctor… not like they turn and say, “Hey, you.” It’s more that what they say, the words they choose, it feels meant for me. Like at the domino table, or on the radio, there are little messages in it. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, but I still feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages are specifically intended for you, and not for anyone else?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to sound foolish, doctor, but yes. It feels like those little messages are meant for me, not just anybody. The men playing dominoes, or sometimes the radio announcer… they say things in a way that I know is warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s only regular talk, and maybe she’s right about some of it, but to me it feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't feel like you were receiving these messages, or has this been a constant feeling for you?\n\nPatient: It’s not every minute, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're receiving these messages?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about nine months, doctor. Is that the kind of time you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe these messages are meant for you even when your wife or others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do, though I know maybe it sounds strange. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try to listen to her, I really do. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer says it a certain way, I still feel like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything… that’s just how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't believe these messages are meant for you at all?\n\nPatient: Well… sometimes I try to tell myself maybe it’s just regular talk, like my wife says. I don’t want to be stubborn about it, doctor. But when it happens—like with the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer says something a certain way—I still feel that it’s meant for me. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you completely stop believing these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try to set it aside when my wife tells me it’s just talk, and I don’t want to make too much of it. But doctor, that’s why I came—those men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they give these little messages, and it feels meant for me. Even if others say it isn’t, I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be unusual or that others have told you aren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s much else, doctor… at least not that I can say for sure. It’s mostly what I already told you, about the men at the domino table and sometimes the radio announcer. They say these little things, and it feels like the messages are meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just talk, and I try not to make a big thing of it… but I do feel they’re warning me to be careful. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other people who are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say… harm me exactly, doctor. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s more that the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, say little things that feel meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I try to listen to her, but I still feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there are other things going on that are specifically about you or directed at you, besides these messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say there’s a lot else, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound strange. It’s mainly those little messages I told you about—the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer. They’ll say something, and it feels like it’s for me, like they’re warning me to be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and I try to listen, but… I still feel it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these beliefs about the messages ever completely go away for a long time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… I mean, I try not to dwell on it, doctor, and sometimes I can go on with my day and not think about it much. But it doesn’t really go away for a long time. When the men at the domino table say those things, or the radio announcer says something, I still feel it’s meant for me… like they’re warning me to be careful, even if my wife tells me it’s just talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think about these messages for several weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Several weeks? No, doctor, I don’t think so… I try not to make too much of it, and some days I keep busy and don’t think on it as much. But it’s still there, you know. The men at the domino table, or sometimes the radio announcer, they say little things and I feel it’s meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and I want to believe her, but I still know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever stop believing that these messages are meant for you?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think maybe I could stop, doctor… I don’t want to be difficult about it. But honestly, when the men at the domino table say those little things, or the radio announcer says something, it still feels meant for me. Even when my wife says it’s just talk, I can’t quite let go of it… I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these messages will continue to feel meant for you in the future?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, doctor, I think they probably will. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but when the men at the domino table say certain things, or the radio announcer says something, it still feels like it’s for me. Even if my wife says it’s just talk, I can’t help feeling they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that these messages aren't actually meant for you?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose there’s a possibility, doctor. I don’t want to sound stubborn. My wife tells me it’s just ordinary talk, and I try to take that in. But when the men at the domino table say certain things, or sometimes the radio announcer does, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re warning me to be careful. So… I can say maybe, but I don’t really believe it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever be able to ignore these messages completely?\n\nPatient: I’d like to be able to, doctor. I really would. I don’t want to keep making something out of it if I’m not supposed to. So completely ignore it? I don’t know if I can, not yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that these messages are meant for you will ever completely disappear?\n\nPatient: I hope it would, doctor… I don’t want to keep troubling anybody with it. Even with my wife saying it’s just talk, I feel like they’re warning me to be careful. Is that… is that the kind of answer you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about these messages will still be the same a year from now?\n\nPatient: A year from now… I’d like to tell you no, doctor, that I’ll be able to let it go.
